# Final Data Dictionary and Master File Inventory

**Project:** Multi-Modal Data Fusion for Automated Accessibility & Usability Scoring

**Date:** 2026-05-27

## 1. Executive Summary

This document consolidates the uploaded CS19 capstone deliverable into a **Master File Inventory**, a **Final Data Dictionary**, **Mapping Rules**, **Output Schema Documentation**, and a concise **Data Pipeline Explanation**. It is based on inspection of 95 project files, including 63 CSV files, notebooks, scripts, narrative outputs, and generated presentation assets. The project implements automated accessibility and usability scoring for the AAMI website user-testing dataset through Model C / Proposal 1, Proposal 2 participant-normalized time moderation, independent Tester Quality Rating, and manual-review validation artifacts.[^readme]

The most important documentation constraint is that the uploaded files do not use a single uniform participant or task schema. Some raw files use survey response identifiers and tester IDs, some processed outputs use `email`, `participant`, and `participant_key`, while transcript segment files are keyed by participant-like filenames. For that reason, this dictionary distinguishes **observed raw fields** from **standardised field names**, and any uncertain meaning is explicitly labelled as **possible meaning based on context**.

| Documentation Area | Result |
|---|---|
| Files inspected | 95 project files |
| Tabular fields profiled | 736 observed file-field occurrences |
| Core scoring layers | Model C / Proposal 1, Proposal 2, Tester Quality, Manual Review validation |
| Key caution | Proposal 2 changes only L3 usability time moderation; accessibility scores are unchanged by Proposal 2.[^readme] |
| Key validation note | Manual review is a validation sample, not official ground truth.[^manual] |

## 3. Final Data Dictionary

The following dictionary uses standardised field names to make the project easier to explain in a final report or client appendix. The **Raw Source Field(s)** column includes only fields observed in the uploaded files. Where a standard field is a conceptual join key or documentation convenience, the table states that it was not directly observed as a column.

| Standard Field Name           | Raw Source Field(s)                                                                                             | Meaning                                                                                                                                   | Data Type                      | Valid Values                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Example                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Used In                                                            |
|:------------------------------|:----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|:------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|:-------------------------------|:--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|:-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|:-------------------------------------------------------------------|
| participant_key               | email, participant, participant_key, tester_id, tester_name, tester_email, TesterFullName                       | Canonical participant join key used in processed scoring; raw sources use multiple identifiers. Possible meaning based on context.        | String / identifier            | participant: examples ghum \| ramazankawish \| rae; participant: examples ghum \| ramazankawish \| rae; email: examples ghum@me.com \| ramazankawish@gmail.com \| ghum@me.com                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Terence Flint; terryaflint17@gmail.com; Lauren Metzler                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | All scoring, Proposal 2, Manual Review, Tester Quality             |
| tester_email                  | email, tester_email                                                                                             | Tester email address when available; used for matching survey, insight, scoring, and review records.                                      | String                         | email: examples ghum@me.com \| ramazankawish@gmail.com \| ghum@me.com; email: examples troyparnell@hotmail.com; email: examples troyparnell@hotmail.com                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | terryaflint17@gmail.com; reneerussell99@hotmail.com; reececraigie@outlook.com                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Model C / Proposal 1 / Proposal 2 / Manual Review / Tester Quality |

Possible meaning based on context when derived from filename or email.                         | String                         | participant: examples ghum \| ramazankawish \| rae; participant: examples ghum \| ramazankawish \| rae; participant: examples ghum \| ramazankawish \| ghum                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Terence Flint; Lauren Metzler; Carl Robinson                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Proposal 2 / Manual Review / Tester Quality                        |

## 4. Mapping Rules

The mapping rules below describe the transformations visible from the uploaded data, notebooks, scripts, and README. These should be treated as documentation of the current capstone package rather than as a universal product schema. In particular, participant and task matching depend on the available keys in each source file.

| Raw Field                                                  | Standard Field                       | Transformation Rule                                                                                                                                                       | Reason                                                                                                       |
|:-----------------------------------------------------------|:-------------------------------------|:--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|:-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Email / email / tester_email                               | participant_key / tester_email       | Trim whitespace; where matching is needed, compare lowercased email-like identifiers. If email is unavailable, use participant name or participant_key only with caution. | The project uses multiple participant identifiers across survey, insights, transcript segments, and outputs. |
| TesterFullName / tester_name / participant                 | participant_name / participant_key   | Standardise spacing and casing; for filename-derived segment keys, convert to a stable slug only when needed.                                                             | Segment files and some outputs use participant handles rather than raw survey IDs.                           |
| task / task_title / Title / model_c_task                   | task_title / model_c_task            | Retain the human-readable title; map to model_c_task_key or task_id when available.                                                                                       | Task labels vary between raw task metadata, Model C labels, and Proposal 2 time-signal tables.               |
| task_id / model_c_task_key                                 | task_id                              | Use explicit task ID where present; otherwise derive a task key from mapped task title only if mapping table/code supports it.                                            | Proposal 2 and manual review require task-level joins.                                                       |
| Survey linear-scale answer_data / raw_score                | raw_survey_score                     | Parse JSON-like answer payloads and extract numeric/normalised value when present; otherwise use existing raw_score fields from scoring exports.                          | Model C starts from participant-reported survey score as a ceiling/baseline.                                 |
| raw_score / ceiling_score                                  | ceiling_score                        | Apply ceiling logic so automated scores cannot exceed the participant-stated survey score.                                                                                | README states scores can only stay the same or go down from the participant stated survey score.             |
| LLM insight category / severity / friction_type            | friction evidence features           | Classify insight evidence into accessibility or usability streams and assign severity/cap effects according to Model C / Proposal 1 notebook logic.                       | Insight evidence is the qualitative input to Model C / Proposal 1 scoring.                                   |
| confidence                                                 | confidence                           | Use source-specific confidence as a supporting signal; do not assume identical scale across insight, time, and manual-review sources.                                     | The same raw column name appears in several contexts.                                                        |
| duration_minutes / task_segmentation_combined windows      | raw_duration / duration_minutes      | Use task segmentation boundaries and transcript segment files to estimate task duration.                                                                                  | Proposal 2 time moderation depends on participant-task time evidence.                                        |
| duration_minutes + participant timing profile              | normalized_duration / speed_factor   | Normalise duration relative to participant-level timing pattern before using it as a signal.                                                                              | This avoids directly penalising participants who are generally slower.                                       |
| normalized_duration + normalized_task_median               | normalized_median_ratio              | Compare participant-normalized task duration against task median.                                                                                                         | Proposal 2 uses this ratio to identify unusually slow task-level evidence.                                   |
| normalized_median_ratio                                    | proposal2_time_cap                   | Apply time-cap thresholds only as Proposal 2 L3 usability moderation; leave accessibility scores unchanged.                                                               | README explicitly states Proposal 2 only changes L3 usability time moderation.                               |
| l3_score_before_time + proposal2_time_cap                  | l3_score_after_proposal2_time        | Take the lower/more conservative value when a cap triggers; no automated score increase is allowed.                                                                       | The project design constraint is monotonic non-increase.                                                     |
| participant × task scoring rows                            | task / participant / project rollups | Aggregate row-level outputs by task, participant, or project using means and counts as exposed in rollup output files.                                                    | Final reporting requires multiple granularities.                                                             |
| manual review selected cases                               | manual validation metrics            | Compare raw survey, Model C before time, and Proposal 2 after time against reviewer-estimated scores; treat findings as validation sample only.                           | Manual review is not official ground truth and is limited in sample size.                                    |
| open-text answer volume and specificity                    | feedback_depth_score                 | Compute quality sub-score from word count, non-empty answers, vague answer count, and actionable/specific issue terms where available.                                    | Tester Quality measures evidence reliability.                                                                |
| tasks attempted / survey responses / transcript evidence   | task_coverage_score                  | Compute coverage ratio and associated sub-score at participant level.                                                                                                     | Tester Quality should reflect whether evidence covers enough tasks.                                          |
| score gap / high-score-with-friction counts                | evidence_consistency_score           | Lower consistency when score patterns conflict with strong friction evidence.                                                                                             | This supports review prioritisation without changing ACC/USA scores.                                         |
| session duration / transcript word count / friction counts | engagement_effort_score              | Use duration and transcript/evidence activity indicators as effort signals, subject to missing-data safeguards.                                                           | Tester Quality is an independent monitoring layer.                                                           |
| review flags and mismatch indicators                       | review_flag                          | Flag uncertain, inconsistent, sparse, or high-risk cases for human review rather than automatic exclusion.                                                                | The project treats review as a confidence/safety layer.                                                      |

## 5. Output Schema Documentation

The project outputs operate at multiple levels of granularity. The most important row-level scoring output is `outputs/proposal2_integrated_l3_time_score.csv`, because it combines Model C / Proposal 1 fields with Proposal 2 time features and after-time usability scores. The Tester Quality outputs are participant-level confidence artifacts and should remain separate from final accessibility/usability scoring.[^readme]

| Output File                                                              | File Type               | Dataset Layer   |   Rows |   Columns | Row Granularity                                            | Scoring Scale / Interpretation                                                                                                                           | Key Fields / Columns                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
|:-------------------------------------------------------------------------|:------------------------|:----------------|-------:|----------:|:-----------------------------------------------------------|:---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|:-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

## 6. Data Pipeline Explanation

The pipeline starts with raw survey, task, assignment, project, transcript-segmentation, and LLM-derived insight files. These sources provide survey responses, task definitions, participant/cohort context, qualitative friction evidence, and approximate task timing. Preprocessing is necessary because the source data uses different identifiers, different answer structures, and mixed file formats. For example, the raw survey score export is named with an `.xls` extension, but the inspected file can be parsed as delimited text rather than as a native Excel workbook.[^readme]

The first scoring stage is **Model C / Proposal 1**. It uses survey scores as a ceiling, routes evidence into accessibility and usability streams, applies cap or deduction logic, and produces row-level accessibility and usability scores. The README states that scores can only stay the same or go down from the participant-stated survey score; this monotonic design is important because it prevents the automated model from inflating a participant’s self-reported rating.[^readme]

The second stage is **Proposal 2 participant-normalized time moderation**. It takes the pre-time L3 usability score and merges time-signal evidence at participant-task level. Raw time is not treated as direct failure evidence. Instead, Proposal 2 uses participant-relative timing features, including normalized duration, task medians, normalized-median ratios, and time caps. Its output summary states that 136 Model C rows were processed, 95 rows matched time evidence, 14 rows triggered normalized time caps, and 12 rows had changed L3 usability scores, with no score increases and no accessibility-score changes.[^proposal2]

The third stage is **Tester Quality Rating**, which is deliberately independent of final accessibility and usability scoring. Tester Quality uses participant-level evidence features such as open-text depth, task coverage, evidence consistency, and engagement/effort indicators. It should be used as a confidence and review-prioritisation layer rather than a multiplier on final scores.[^readme]

The fourth stage is **Manual Review validation**. The validation sample compares raw survey score, Model C before-time score, and Proposal 2 after-time score against reviewer-estimated manual scores. The uploaded findings emphasise that the manual review is a validation sample rather than official ground truth, and that the sample does not support a broad claim that Proposal 2 is always more accurate. Model C before time had the lowest mean absolute error in the reviewed sample, while Proposal 2 was useful in specific cases but noisy when long durations reflected success, verbose narration, multi-route exploration, or approximate segmentation boundaries.[^manual]

The final outputs are therefore best understood as a layered evidence system rather than a single black-box score. Row-level scoring tables support auditability, rollup tables support reporting, manual-review files support validation, and Tester Quality files support confidence monitoring. This separation should be preserved in the final demo, final report appendix, and any client-facing explanation.

## 7. Notes / Assumptions / Unknown Fields

| Item | Note |
|---|---|
| Participant identifiers | There is no single universal participant ID column across all uploaded files. The project uses `email`, `participant`, `participant_key`, `tester_id`, `tester_name`, and filename-derived segment keys depending on file layer. |
| Assignment file | `aami-website-assignments(2).csv` is a lightweight roster/cohort file with tester name, opt-in status, and cohort codes; it is not a task-assignment matrix in the inspected package. |
| Raw answer payloads | `answer_data` is JSON-like text whose structure depends on `question_type`; parsing rules should be documented in code if this package is extended. |
| Cohort code meanings | Cohort codes such as `ND`, `OT`, `ESL`, `LV`, `BL`, `DF`, and `OL` appear in raw data, but their formal definitions are not fully documented in the uploaded files. |
| Manual review | Manual review scores are reviewer-estimated anchors for validation only; they should not be represented as official ground truth. |
| Proposal 2 | Time evidence is a supporting usability signal. It should not be used to modify accessibility scores or to infer failure without supporting evidence. |
| Tester Quality | Tester Quality is an independent confidence layer and must not be multiplied into final accessibility or usability outputs. |
| Schema drift | Some reference scripts expect column names or spaced filenames that differ from the inspected raw files. This document prioritises observed uploaded fields over inferred script assumptions. |
